[04:37:03] Ugur Keser | Message #72215
Edited: 2026-09-18 04:37:35
DBBI is the first 91 characters of the SalPhaseIon stream, drawn from a nine-letter alphabet a–i. You can't parse it on its own, because the parser's parameters live in the picture: the rabbit image is a 14×14 grid read in spiral order, and every 8th cell is coloured — those colours are the last bit of each byte of the URL gsmg.io/theseedisplanted, blue for 1, yellow for 0. Line those 24 colours up in spiral order, splice in the single off-white FEFEFE cell at spiral position 164 as the 21st symbol, and you have a 25-symbol program. You run it like this: walk logical positions starting at 1; if the position isn't prime you consume one character from the stream and call it filler, and if it is prime you look at the next colour symbol and consume 1 character for blue or 2 for yellow, calling that chunk a "host". There are 23 primes up to 83, eight of them yellow; 83 logical steps plus the 8 extra characters the yellows eat comes to exactly 91, so the stream is consumed precisely — nothing left over, nothing short. And here's the verification: all 23 hosts start with b, and every yellow host is exactly be. On a shuffled DBBI that lands around 6.9/23 on average, and across 200,000 shuffles it never once hits 23/23 — p < 5×10⁻⁶. Drop FEFEFE from the program and you fall to 21/23; treat it as yellow instead of blue and you also get 21/23, so that cell isn't decoration, it's a required part of the program. The upshot is that DBBI is a second copy of the picture's colour sequence written in a completely different encoding; it carries no new payload of its own, it behaves like a checksum telling you that you're holding the right key.
Reactions: 🤡 x1

[05:57:55] Doober | Message #72285
Yes there is, cosmic duality is a salted container, same has every other one with 8 byte header and 8 byte Salted__ has been, needs a perfect input but since OpenSSL encription gives 1/256 a false positive so if you test enough of those you will get the false positives. Also, as the creator said, it's 2 hours max or same day solve once you crack it

[05:59:56] Doober | Message #72286
Edited: 2026-09-21 14:23:34
Cosmic Duality is already an OpenSSL-style salted container. The decoded bytes begin with the literal Salted__, followed by an 8-byte salt and then ciphertext.

1. Valid PKCS#7 padding
A wrong AES-CBC password/key produces plaintext that is effectively random bytes. Random bytes can accidentally have valid PKCS#7 padding.
For AES's 16-byte block:
\[ P(\text{valid padding}) = \frac1{256}+\frac1{256^2}+\cdots+\frac1{256^{16}} \approx \frac1{255} \]
So roughly 0.39% of wrong decryptions, about 1 in 255–256, will look like they have valid padding.

[06:00:19] Doober | Message #72287
So if you open it and it doesn't have any discernible understandible text like this puzzle has produced during the solve path, then it's most likely padding.

[07:19:47] E | Message #73787
Edited: 2026-09-28 21:26:35
A: infrared (hint) - before Salphaseion

B: "we wont give away the password its in front of your eyes but youre not seeing it" (later hint) - after Salphaseion

A is one way to answer B (also a better way to explain how to look as well)

Go back to the first puzzle piece (rabbit grid)
-- count the colors in the image: 
--- not counting the color gradient "G" (GSMG logo on image)

        7 main colors 

"7 intertwined passwords" (archichoice text)

PNG image uses 4 color channels
-- RGBA
--- each channel is is represented by 2 hex characters
---- total hex for each channel is 8 (alpha is always "ff")

7 * 8 = 56

private key needs 64 (we are missing 8)

"BRUTEFORCE may be required" (archichoice text)

1 ffffffff WHT
2 000000ff BLK
3 3f48ccff BLU
4 fff200ff YEL
5 fefefeff WHT2
6 ed1c24ff RED
7 f5f5f5ff WHT3 (under red line)

8 ??? (bruteforce may be required)

example:

ffffffff000000ff3f48ccfffff200fffefefeffed1c24fff5f5f5ff????????

7 intertwined colors (in front of your eyes)

read in Hex (the part your not seeing)

bruteforce may be required (find the correct order of colors to hex & brute the missing 8 characters)

Is it right? No idea....

But it is possible and not really so complicated as to be outside of reality when put up against what was needed to solve what we have so far... 

Anyways just dropping an idea if any are interested in checking out

As always, good luck everyone

[08:09:43] Андрей Александрович | Message #73793
Edited: 2026-09-27 08:15:43
We got ANTIYBCORE from the DBBI branch like this:

1. We mapped the DBBI alphabet by first appearance:



D=0, B=1, I=2, F=3, H=4, C=5, E=6, G=7, A=8

2. We placed the 91-character DBBI string into its natural 7×13 grid and summed each column. That gives:



[19, 27, 26, 34, 13, 38, 11, 31, 25, 37, 18, 27, 33]

3. We treated those 13 numbers as zero-based “nth word from the end” indices applied to the Architect text immediately before the modified SELECT section.



That selects these words:

A / NOW / TO / IT / YOU / BECOME / CARRY / OF / RETURN / EXCEEDINGLY / TEMPORARY / NOW / THE

4. Taking the first letter of each selected word gives:



A N T I Y B C O R E T N T

So the output is:

ANTIYBCORETNT

and the striking prefix is:

ANTIYBCORE

This was interesting because ANTI, YB, and CORE all matched structures we were already seeing elsewhere: anti-diagonal geometry, Yellow/Blue, and the 84-cell interior/core.

Important caveat: the extraction is fully reproducible, and random-shuffle controls made the ANTIYBCORE prefix unusually strong, but it is still not creator-authenticated because the “take initials of the selected words” step is inferred rather than explicitly instructed.

[08:59:43] sol | Message #74864
1.XOR — exclusive OR
Meaning: Exactly one is true, but not both.
Example: “You may enter with a ticket XOR a wristband.”
Ticket only → ✅ yes
Wristband only → ✅ yes
Both → ❌ no
Neither → ❌ no
So XOR is like: “one or the other, but not both.”

2. “and/or”
Meaning: Inclusive OR. It means one, the other, or both.
Example: “Bring a pen and/or paper.”
Pen only → ✅ yes
Paper only → ✅ yes
Both pen and paper → ✅ yes
Neither → ❌ no
